India’s energy transition is entering a defining phase. As renewable capacity expands 
rapidly, the next challenge is not only to generate more clean energy, but also to build 
the domestic manufacturing ecosystem required to support it. Achieving India’s 
ambition of 500 GW of non-fossil fuel capacity by 2030 will therefore depend on 
stronger local capabilities that enhance energy security, reduce import dependence, 
and build long-term industrial competitiveness.
Saatvik’s New Energy Blueprint is shaped around this opportunity. It is a long-term strategy 
to progressively strengthen the Company’s position across the renewable energy value chain 
through greater manufacturing scale, deeper integration, and a broader portfolio of clean 
energy solutions.
The Company is scaling 
to 6 GW of solar cells, 
8.8 GW of modules, 
6 GW of ingots and 
wafers and 5 GW of 
Encapsulant capacity, 
building an integrated 
manufacturing presence 
across the solar value 
chain.Backward integration begins 
with 6 GW of solar cells 
manufacturing in two phases, 
extending to a 6 GW ingots 
and wafers roadmap, and 
supported by encapsulant 
capacity scaling from 2 GW 
to 5 GW, feeding 8.8 GW of 
cumulative modules capacity, 
alongside EPC and IPP 
capabilities.Beyond ingots, wafers 
solar cells, modules and 
Encapsulant manufacturing, 
Saatvik offers uday series 
inverters, transformers through 
Melcon, solar pumps, solar kits, 
and turnkey EPC with lifecycle 
operations and maintenance. 
Furthermore, Battery energy 
Storage Systems are in pipeline.Manufacturing capacity 
to support India’s 
growing renewable 
energy demand.Greater control across 
manufacturing through 
backward integration into 
critical capabilities.Expansion into complementary 
energy solutions that strengthen 
customer offerings and create 
multiple long-term growth 
opportunities.Scale Integration Diversification
Together, these strategic priorities are shaping a more integrated, diversified, and future-ready 
renewable energy company, positioned to participate in India’s next phase of clean energy growth.
Mr. Neelesh Garg,  Chairman and Managing DirectorIndia’s energy transition will not only be installed 
in India; it must be manufactured in India. Saatvik 
is building the integrated platform to help make 
that possible.THE

solar cells and modules.The Company commissioned its

control over encapsulant, a critical 
material used in solar modules

to 20 GW over the next five years.
Why it Matters
Creates a new growth platform with 
potential to scale alongside rising 
demand for grid-scale energy storage.

Building the Energy Enterprise 
of TomorrowABOUT SAATVIK GREEN ENERGY LIMITED
Saatvik Green Energy Limited 
(‘Saatvik’ or ‘The Company’) 
is an Indian renewable energy 
equipment manufacturer 
and turnkey solar solutions 
provider. Its operations 
are anchored in two core 
businesses: manufacturing 
high-efficiency solar 
photovoltaic (PV) modules and 
executing end-to-end solar 
Engineering, Procurement and 
Construction (EPC) projects.
Annual Report 2025-26
4
4 Annual Report 2025-26 4
=== page 8 ===
Incorporated in 2015, Saatvik has evolved into an 
increasingly integrated clean energy platform, combining 
high-efficiency solar PV modules, in-house encapsulants, 
EPC capabilities and battery energy storage solutions. 
Through this expanding ecosystem, the Company is 
strengthening its participation across the renewable energy 
value chain while contributing to India’s transition towards 
net-zero emissions and extending its reach across 
global markets.
From its flagship manufacturing campus in Ambala, 
Haryana, Saatvik currently operates 4.8 GW of annual 
modules production capacity. Since inception, the Company 
has supplied over 6 GW of modules across utility-scale, 
commercial and industrial, residential rooftop, agricultural 
and distributed energy applications, serving customers 
across India and more than 20 countries.
Complementing its manufacturing operations, Saatvik’s EPC 
division delivers end-to-end solar infrastructure solutions. 
The combination of in-house modules manufacturing and 
project execution capabilities enables the Company to offer 
customers an integrated, single-point solution for clean 
energy deployment.Saatvik is further strengthening this platform through 
targeted expansion across both upstream and downstream 
segments.
Upstream  
The 2 GW encapsulant facility at Ambala caters to 
internal requirements while also supplying other 
manufacturers. At Gopalpur, Odisha, 6 GW of solar 
cells manufacturing capacity is under ramp up, 
with a further 6 GW of ingots and wafers capacity 
planned.
Downstream  
The UDAY Series extends Saatvik’s presence into 
power electronics, Melcon Transformers and 
Electricals Private Limited adds power transmission 
equipment capabilities, while Saatvik Power 
Storage Solutions Limited expands the portfolio into 
battery energy storage.
Together, these capabilities create 
a more integrated operating 
platform, strengthening Saatvik’s 
position beyond modules 
manufacturing and preparing the 
Company to participate across 
a broader spectrum of India’s 
evolving clean energy ecosystem.
Saatvik Green Energy Limited

Purpose
At Saatvik Solar, we exist to accelerate the 
transition towards a clean, sustainable, and 
energy-secure future.
Mission
To deliver zero-defect, technology-led renewable energy solutions that accelerate global 
decarbonisation by building a fully integrated, carbon-neutral enterprise powered by patented 
R&D, digital efficiency, and global manufacturing. We aim to be a trusted clean-energy brand 
for consumers, a benchmark in service leadership, and an employer of choice by advancing 
innovation, quality, and sustainable impact worldwide.
Vision
To be amidst the world’s most 
trusted and admired clean 
energy companies, empowering 
a sustainable future through 
decarbonisation solutions.

Connecting the Energy Value ChainSAATVIK’S CAPABILITIES ACROSS THE ENERGY ECOSYSTEM
Saatvik is progressively expanding beyond solar modules manufacturing by strengthening upstream manufacturing 
capabilities and extending downstream into power electronics, energy storage, power infrastructure and project 
execution. This integrated model strengthens supply-chain resilience, expands the breadth of customer solutions 
and enables the Company to participate across a wider span of the renewable energy value chain

The Vision behind the BlueprintCHAIRMAN’S MESSAGE
“FY 2025-26 marked a 
defining year in Saatvik 
Green Energy Limited’s 
journey. As the Company 
entered the public markets, it 
strengthened  
the foundations of its  
long-term vision through 
strategic investments that 
are progressively expanding 
its role across the renewable 
energy value chain. ”
Dear Shareholders,
FY 2025-26 was a defining year in 
Saatvik Green Energy Limited’s 
evolution. It was a year in which scale, 
integration and institutional maturity 
came together. As India accelerates 
towards a cleaner, more secure and 
increasingly self-reliant energy future, 
we advanced our ambition of building 
Saatvik into an integrated renewable 
energy enterprise with capabilities 
spanning manufacturing, power 
electronics, energy storage, project 
execution and energy infrastructure.
The investments made during the 
year were therefore not isolated 
additions to the business. They were 
deliberate steps towards creating 
greater depth across the energy value 
chain, strengthening control over critical capabilities and expanding 
the opportunities in which Saatvik 
can participate. From deepening 
backward integration to building 
complementary downstream 
businesses, our focus has been on 
creating an enterprise that is more 
resilient, more competitive  
and better positioned to serve the 
evolving requirements of India’s  
energy ecosystem.
Our successful listing on the National 
Stock Exchange of India Limited and 
BSE Limited added another important 
dimension to this transformation. 
Entering the public markets has 
broadened our stakeholder base 
and strengthened the institutional 
responsibilities that accompany 
growth. The confidence placed in 
Saatvik by our shareholders is both an 
endorsement of the journey so far and 
an expectation of what must follow. 
As we move forward, we remain 
committed to disciplined capital 
allocation, transparent governance 
and the creation of enduring value.  
On behalf of the Board of Directors,  
I thank you for your trust and 
continued support.The Opportunity Ahead
The global economy continues to 
navigate geopolitical uncertainty, 
realigning supply chains and 
shifting trade dynamics. Yet, the 
long-term case for clean energy 
remains compelling. Energy security 
has become a strategic priority, 
manufacturing supply chains are 
being reassessed, and governments 
and industries continue to invest 
in renewable energy capacity and 
domestic manufacturing capabilities.
Against this backdrop, the renewable 
energy industry is entering a more 
mature phase of development. 
Competitiveness will increasingly 
depend not only on scale, but also on 
manufacturing depth, technological 
capability, supply chain resilience  
and the ability to offer solutions 
across a broader part of the energy 
value chain.
Customer requirements are evolving 
as well. Increasingly, customers 
are looking beyond standalone 
products towards partners capable 
of supporting projects across

engineering, execution and lifecycle 
requirements. In parallel, policy 
measures in India continue to

These shifts reinforce the strategic 
direction adopted by Saatvik. By 
strengthening its manufacturing

Where Saatvik is Heading
As the energy landscape evolves, 
the Company’s ambition continues 
to evolve with it. The next phase of 
Saatvik’s growth is anchored in three 
strategic priorities.
The first is to strengthen 
manufacturing competitiveness 
through backward integration 
into critical components and 
technologies. This is expected to 
deepen value addition, improve  
supply-chain resilience and provide 
greater control over key inputs.
The second is to expand into 
complementary businesses across 
the renewable energy ecosystem. 
Building capabilities in adjacent 
segments will enable the Company 
to address a wider range of customer 
requirements, deepen relationships 
and create additional avenues  
for growth.
The third is to strengthen execution 
capabilities across engineering, 
project delivery and lifecycle services. 
This will enable the Company to 
participate across a larger share of 
the renewable energy value chain and 
offer customers more comprehensive 
solutions.Together, these priorities provide 
a clear framework for Saatvik’s 
long-term evolution. The objective 
is not expansion for its own sake, 
but disciplined growth anchored 
in operational excellence, capital 
efficiency and sustainable  
value creation.
Creating Value 
Responsibly
Growth of this scale must be 
supported by equal discipline in the 
way the Company is governed and 
managed. Saatvik remains committed 
to strong governance, prudent risk 
management and an organisational 
culture grounded in ethics, 
transparency and accountability.
During the year, the Company 
published its inaugural Business 
Responsibility and Sustainability 
Report, marking an important step 
towards strengthening sustainability 
disclosures and stakeholder 
engagement. The constitution 
of the ESG Council has further 
institutionalised oversight by creating 
a structured mechanism to review 
sustainability priorities, regulatory 
developments and stakeholder 
expectations, while integrating 
environmental, social and governance 
considerations into strategic 
decision-making.
The Company’s continued focus 
on responsible manufacturing, 
environmental stewardship, 
workplace safety and sound 
governance reflects a clear principle: 
enduring value is created not only 
by how a business grows, but also 
by how responsibly that growth is 
achieved.
Warm Regards,
Neelesh Garg

India’s renewable energy 
transition represents one of the 
most consequential industrial 
transformations of this era.  
The companies that will create 
enduring value are those that can 
effectively combine manufacturing 
scale with technological depth, 
disciplined execution, and the 
agility to evolve in step with a rapidly 
maturing energy ecosystem.
Saatvik enters its next phase from 
a position of enhanced strength, 
underpinned by an expanding 
manufacturing footprint, a 
strengthened balance sheet, and a 
clearly defined strategic roadmap. 
The investments being undertaken 
today are directed towards building 
capabilities that will remain relevant 
and value-accretive well beyond the 
current growth cycle.
As Saatvik advances its long-term 
vision, the Board remains steadfast 
in its commitment to responsible 
leadership, prudent capital 
allocation, robust governance 
standards, and the creation of 
sustainable shareholder value. 
On behalf of the Board of Directors, 
we extend our sincere gratitude to 
our shareholders for their continued 
trust, to our customers and 
business partners for their enduring 
confidence, and to our employees 
for their dedication and contribution.
Saatvik remains committed to 
playing a meaningful role in India’s 
clean energy transition, while 
continuing to build a stronger, 
more integrated, and more resilient 
enterprise for the future.

Scaling the Blueprint ResponsiblyMANAGING DIRECTOR’S MESSAGE 
“Saatvik enters its next phase 
of growth with a stronger 
balance sheet, a deeper 
manufacturing footprint and 
a clear roadmap  
for integration. ”
Dear Shareholders,
FY 2025-26 was a year in which strategy 
translated into action. We scaled 
operations, strengthened the balance 
sheet, deployed capital into critical 
manufacturing capabilities and laid 
the groundwork for a more integrated 
business model. Each decision was 
taken with a clear objective: to improve 
the quality of growth while building an 
enterprise capable of compounding 
value over the long term.
As we prepare for the next phase of 
expansion, capital discipline remains 
central to how we manage the business. 
We are investing in manufacturing 
scale, backward integration and 
complementary businesses, but with a 
clear focus on returns, balance-sheet 
resilience and execution. Our objective 
is not simply to become larger. It is to 
build a business with greater control 
over its value chain, stronger earnings 
potential and the financial flexibility to 
sustain growth across cycles.A Year of Strong Financial 
Performance
The progress made during the year 
was reflected in the strongest financial 
performance in Saatvik’s history.
Revenue from operations increased  
111% to INR 45,484 million, supported 
by higher production, improved capacity 
utilisation and sustained customer 
demand. EBITDA stood at  
INR 5,811 million, while Profit After 
Tax grew 64% to INR 3,571 million. 
Importantly, this growth was  
accompanied by a meaningful 
strengthening of the balance sheet, with 
the debt-to-equity ratio improving from 
1.34x to 0.65x following the IPO.
Margins moderated during the year as 
industry pricing normalised and solar cells 
procurement costs remained elevated. 
This needs to be seen alongside the scale 
of investment currently underway. A 
meaningful share of capital is deployed in manufacturing assets that are yet to 
commence commercial production. As 
these facilities are commissioned and 
utilisation rises, they are expected to 
improve operating leverage, increase 
internal value addition and support 
stronger profitability over time.
As of March 31, 2026, approximately 
56% of the IPO proceeds had been 
deployed towards manufacturing 
expansion, backward integration 
and debt reduction, in line with the 
stated objectives of the public issue. 
In FY 2026-27, we plan to invest 
approximately INR 17,000 million, 
primarily in the Odisha integrated 
manufacturing project. This programme 
will be funded through a balanced mix 
of internal accruals and debt, while 
remaining within our targeted gearing 
range.
Capital Allocation with a 
Long-Term Perspective
Capital allocation is one of 
management’s most consequential 
responsibilities, particularly at a stage 
when the business is scaling rapidly. 
Every investment is therefore assessed 
not only for its standalone return 
potential, but also for the strategic 
advantage it can create for the broader 
enterprise.
During the year, we continued to 
allocate capital towards manufacturing 
expansion, backward integration 
and adjacent businesses that extend 
Saatvik’s participation across the 
renewable energy value chain. The 
approach remains phased and 
deliberate, allowing us to pursue growth 
while retaining financial flexibility and 
maintaining appropriate leverage.
The improvement in our debt-to-equity 
ratio provides greater room to fund 
this next phase without compromising 
balance-sheet resilience. As these 
investments become operational,  
we expect them to improve 
manufacturing economics, reduce

Building an Integrated 
Manufacturing Advantage
A significant part of this investment is 
directed towards creating greater depth 
within our manufacturing platform.
During FY 2025-26, we advanced the 
integrated manufacturing project at 
Gopalpur, Odisha, while expanding 
the scope of our backward integration 
roadmap. Planned solar cells 
manufacturing capacity was increased 
from 4.8 GW to 6 GW, reflecting the 
growing opportunity for domestic solar 
cells manufacturing and the policy 
environment supporting greater local 
value addition. We also announced 
plans for 6 GW of ingots and wafers 
manufacturing capacity, extending 
integration further upstream.
At Ambala, the commissioning of 
the 2 GW EPE encapsulant facility 
marked another important step. By 
bringing a critical input in-house, 
we are strengthening supply-chain 
resilience while creating an additional 
manufacturing capability that can  
serve both internal requirements and 
external customers.
Taken together, these investments 
are intended to give Saatvik greater 
control over critical inputs, improve 
manufacturing integration and 
strengthen the economics of the core 
business. Importantly, they are being 
implemented in phases aligned with 
demand visibility, capital availability 
and our broader growth roadmap.
Expanding beyond 
Manufacturing
Our strategy, however, is not confined 
to deeper manufacturing integration. 
The renewable energy market is 
increasingly moving towards broader 
solution requirements, and customers are looking for partners capable of 
supporting more of the project lifecycle.
In April 2026 we acquired an  
80% stake in Melcon Transformers and 
Electricals Private Limited, establishing 
a presence in power transmission 
equipment manufacturing. The launch 
of the UDAY Series of on-grid inverters 
expanded our power electronics 
portfolio, while the incorporation 
of Saatvik Power Storage Solutions 
Limited marked our entry into battery 
energy storage systems, an area that 
will become increasingly relevant as 
renewable penetration rises and grid 
flexibility becomes more important.
Our solar pump business also 
progressed during the year, supported 
by government programmes and rising 
adoption of decentralised renewable 
energy solutions. These businesses 
are strategically adjacent to our core. 
They allow us to address a wider set 
of customer requirements, participate 
across a larger share of project value 
and build additional revenue streams 
around the manufacturing platform 
already in place.
Advancing Our ESG 
Commitment
FY 2025-26 marked a significant 
milestone in Saatvik’s ESG journey. 
For the year, the Company prepared 
its first Business Responsibility and 
Sustainability Report, strengthened 
governance through a comprehensive 
suite of Board-approved ESG policies, 
and established an ESG Council 
led by the Chief Executive Officer. 
These measures translated Saatvik’s 
sustainability commitments into 
focused action across resource 
efficiency, climate and water 
stewardship, circularity, product 
responsibility, workplace safety, human 
rights and responsible sourcing. By 
embedding ESG considerations into 
governance, strategic decision-making 
and day-to-day operations, Saatvik is building a resilient and responsible 
foundation for scaling its clean-energy 
manufacturing footprint.
The Way Forward
India’s renewable energy transition 
is creating an opportunity that 
extends far beyond the addition of 
generation capacity. It encompasses 
manufacturing, storage, power 
electronics, transmission infrastructure, 
project execution and an increasingly 
complex domestic supply chain.
Saatvik is positioning itself to participate 
across this broader opportunity with 
a stronger financial base, a more 
integrated manufacturing platform and 
a growing portfolio of complementary 
businesses. The investments underway 
today are intended to improve the 
quality of the business as much as its 
scale, by increasing value addition, 
widening revenue opportunities and 
strengthening resilience.
The confidence placed in us by our 
shareholders brings with it a clear 
responsibility: to deploy capital 
carefully, execute consistently and build 
value that endures beyond individual 
growth cycles. That responsibility will 
continue to guide how we invest and 
how we scale.
On behalf of the management, I thank 
our shareholders for their continued 
trust, our customers and business 
partners for their confidence, and our 
employees for the commitment they 
bring to Saatvik every day.
We enter the next phase with ambition, 
but equally with clarity on what 
must underpin it: sound economics, 
disciplined execution and a business 
model built to become stronger  
with scale.
Warm Regards,
Manik Garg  
Managing Director

Executing the Blueprint with ExcellenceCEO’S MESSAGE
“FY 2025-26 was a year of  
translating ambition into  
execution. Record production,  
stronger capacity utilisation, 
progress across critical 
projects and the addition of  
new capabilities moved Saatvik  
closer to the integrated

Dear Shareholders,
The strength of any blueprint lies in  
how well it is executed. During  
FY 2025-26, we translated strategic intent 
into measurable operating progress 
by scaling manufacturing, advancing 
key expansion projects and building 
capabilities that extend Saatvik’s presence 
across the renewable energy value chain.
That progress was visible across the 
organisation. Manufacturing volumes 
reached record levels, capacity utilisation 
improved, critical projects advanced, 
new businesses were established and 
customer confidence continued to 
deepen. Together, these developments 
reflect more than a year of growth. They 
demonstrate our ability to scale the 
business while strengthening the systems, 
processes and execution capabilities 
required for the next phase.
As Saatvik enters its first full phase as 
a listed organisation, the standard of 
execution must rise with the scale of our ambition. Delivering safely, efficiently 
and on schedule, maintaining 
consistent quality and strengthening 
operating discipline across every part 
of the business remain central to how 
we create sustainable value.
Delivering Consistent 
Operational Performance
FY 2025-26 marked the strongest 
operating performance in Saatvik’s 
history, supported by sustained 
demand, higher manufacturing 
throughput and focused execution.Production increased to 3,162 MW during 
the year, while sales reached 3,138 MW, 
both representing record levels for the 
Company. Capacity utilisation at the Ambala 
manufacturing facility, the Company’s only 
operating facility during FY 2025-26, improved 
to 84.07% against its installed modules 
manufacturing capacity of 4.8 GW. This 
performance demonstrates the strength of our 
production planning, operational discipline 
and manufacturing efficiency. The fourth 
quarter was particularly encouraging, with 
production of 935 MW and sales of  
1,050 MW, reflecting sustained momentum 
through the year. The Company also 
continued to advance its Odisha integrated 
manufacturing project, comprising a phased 
solar cells expansion programme and 4 GW of 
modules capacity. While Phase I includes  
2.4 GW of solar cells manufacturing capacity 
nearing commissioning, Phase II is planned to 
add a further 3.6 GW, taking the total planned 
solar cells manufacturing capacity in Odisha 
to 6 GW.
Behind these numbers lies sustained 
improvement across manufacturing processes, 
quality management, supply planning and 
cross-functional coordination. As the business 
scales, consistency becomes as important 
as capacity. Our teams therefore remain 
focused on strengthening the operating 
systems that enable higher throughput without 
compromising quality, safety or reliability.
Executing the Blueprint,  
One Capability at a Time
Throughout FY 2025-26, our focus remained on 
converting strategic investments into operating 
capability while maintaining the standards that 
customers associate with Saatvik.
Execution, for us, extends well beyond 
commissioning a facility or adding capacity. It 
is reflected in how manufacturing systems are 
scaled, how supply chains are strengthened, 
how new technologies are industrialised and 
how projects are delivered safely, efficiently 
and on schedule.
As Saatvik expands across the renewable 
energy value chain, this discipline becomes 
even more important. Every new capability 
must be supported by the right processes, Operational Highlights

Strengthening

As of March 31, 2026, the Company’s

Customer expectations are evolving 
alongside the market. Increasingly, value

but by the reliability of delivery, technical

to support customers across the project

Our focus therefore extends beyond

remain central to strengthening Saatvik’s

PERC and N-TOPCon technologies, 
supported by continued product

enables us to respond to evolving 
requirements around efficiency, reliability

Building an Organisation 
Ready for Growth
Sustained execution depends as much 
on organisational capability as it does on 
infrastructure and technology. As Saatvik 
expands its manufacturing footprint and 
enters new businesses, preparing our 
people for greater scale and complexity 
remains a key priority.During the year, we continued investing in 
employee learning, leadership development 
and technical capability. Through the Nav 
Urja Kaushal initiative, young professionals 
received hands-on manufacturing training 
while earning Skill India certifications, 
helping create a pipeline of skilled talent to 
support future expansion.
Building a workplace where people feel 
safe, respected and empowered remains 
fundamental to our culture. I am pleased  
to share that Saatvik maintained a zero  
Lost Time Injury Frequency Rate for the 
second consecutive year and received 
the Golden Peacock Occupational Health 
and Safety Award 2026 in the Power 
(Renewable) sector.
We were also recognised as a Great Place 
to Work® for the second consecutive 
year, reflecting our continued focus on 
building an inclusive, collaborative and 
performance-oriented workplace.
These achievements matter because 
execution ultimately depends on people. 
The stronger their capabilities, ownership 
and engagement, the stronger the 
organisation becomes.
Operationalising 
Sustainability
At Saatvik, sustainability is increasingly 
embedded in how we operate rather  
than treated as a parallel agenda.  
Greater manufacturing efficiency, 
responsible resource use and stronger 
process control all contribute towards 
building a more efficient and  
responsible enterprise.
During FY 2025-26, we advanced our 
environmental performance through 
responsible water management, Zero  
Liquid Discharge practices, waste  
reduction and greater resource efficiency 
across operations.
The publication of a verified Environmental 
Product Declaration for the N-TOPCon G12R 
modules provides greater transparency 
around product-level environmental 
performance, while the EcoVadis  
Bronze Medal offers external recognition of the progress being made across 
responsible business practices.
We believe operational excellence and 
sustainability reinforce one another. 
Better resource utilisation, lower waste 
and stronger environmental management 
improve not only the responsibility of 
the business, but also its long-term 
competitiveness.
Looking Ahead
As we enter FY 2026-27, execution will 
remain our defining priority. Advancing 
the commissioning of new manufacturing 
facilities, improving operating efficiencies, 
strengthening product quality and  
delivering consistently superior  
customer experience will continue to  
guide our efforts.
The foundations built during  
FY 2025-26 have expanded the scale 
of what Saatvik can become. The 
responsibility now is to convert that 
potential into performance.
Every facility commissioned, every 
process improved, every product 
delivered and every customer relationship 
strengthened will determine the quality 
of our next phase of growth. As the 
organisation becomes broader and more 
integrated, we will remain focused on 
ensuring that our execution standards 
become stronger with it.
We have laid out the blueprint. The task 
ahead is to execute it with precision, 
consistency and excellence, and to 
translate a broader platform into a 
stronger, more resilient business.
I extend my sincere appreciation to 
our customers for their continued 
confidence, our business partners for 
their collaboration, our shareholders 
for their trust and every member of the 
Saatvik family for their commitment and 
contribution throughout the year.

Nurturing Communities around Operations
Established on June 21, 2022, the Saatvik Social Foundation serves as the CSR arm of the Saatvik Group, advancing initiatives 
across education, healthcare, renewable energy access, livelihoods and community development. Its interventions also extend 
to rural infrastructure, hunger relief and environmental sustainability. With a focus on communities around Ambala and across 
Haryana, the Foundation works to improve quality of life, strengthen local capabilities and address priority social needs, with 
healthcare remaining a key area of emphasis.

1.  GLOBAL ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
 The global economy maintained moderate growth in  
FY 2025-26, navigating persistent geopolitical tensions, 
volatile commodity markets, and evolving trade 
policies. Advanced economies saw steady but subdued 
expansion, while emerging markets – particularly in Asia 
– continued to outperform. The International Monetary 
Fund (IMF) projects global GDP growth of approximately 
3.2%-3.3% for 2025, supported by resilient consumer 
demand and stabilising financial conditions.
 Global inflation continued its gradual moderation, 
easing pressures on central bank policy rates. The US 
Federal Reserve held rates steady through much of FY 
2025-26 before initiating modest cuts, while the ECB 
and Bank of England followed suit. In this environment, 
risk appetite recovered, and capital flows to emerging 
markets – including India – improved.
 The energy transition remained a defining global trend 
FY 2025 with clean energy investments reaching record 
levels. Solar PV continued to lead investment across 
energy technologies, supported by cost competitiveness, 
favourable policy frameworks, and growing power 
demand from electrification, data centres, and AI-driven 
applications.
2.  INDIA’S ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
 India emerged as the world’s fastest-growing major 
economy in FY 2025-26, with GDP growth estimated at 
approximately 6.7%, underpinned by robust domestic 
consumption, infrastructure-led government spending, 
and a buoyant services sector. India’s per capita income 
continued its upward trajectory, and the country’s 
structural fundamentals – young demographics, digital 
adoption, and formalisation of the economy – continue 
to attract global capital.
 Inflation remained broadly within the RBI’s 2-6% target 
range throughout FY 2025-26. CPI inflation eased 
to approximately 3.8-4.2% on an annualised basis, 
supported by stable food prices and government 
interventions The RBI continued its calibrated monetary 
easing during FY 2025-26, lowering the repo rate over 
the course of the year, improving credit availability and 
reducing borrowing costs for businesses.
 Bank credit growth remained strong at approximately 
13-14% YoY , led by services and industrial sectors. 
Government capital expenditure continued at a robust 
pace, with infrastructure spend in roads, railways, 
ports, and clean energy forming the backbone of the 
Capex cycle. The Union Budget FY 2025-26 allocated approximately INR 26,549 Crores (~US$ 3.2 billions) to 
the renewable energy sector, demonstrating sustained 
policy commitment to India’s clean energy vision.
 India’s medium-term economic trajectory remains 
focused on sustaining high economic growth through 
manufacturing-led industrialisation, infrastructure 
development, and structural reforms. The Government 
continues to pursue its long-term ambition of building 
a US$ 5 trillion economy, while initiatives aimed at 
increasing manufacturing's contribution to GDP and 
strengthening domestic supply chains are expected to 
support long-term economic resilience.
3.  RENEWABLE ENERGY SECTOR OVERVIEW
3.1  Global Renewable Energy Landscape
 Global renewable energy capacity additions 
reached a record level of nearly 700 GW in 2024, 
with Solar PV accounting for over 75% of new 
installations—the highest annual addition on 
record. The International Energy Agency (IEA) 
projects renewables to become the world's largest 
source of electricity generation by the end of 2025, 
supplying more than one-third of global electricity. 
Over the remainder of the decade, renewables are 
expected to meet more than 90% of incremental 
global electricity demand.
 Solar PV continued to record robust growth in 
2024, accounting for approximately 7% of global 
electricity generation. Global electricity demand 
increased by approximately 4.3% year-on-year, 
driven by rising temperatures, accelerating 
electrification, and the rapid expansion of data 
centres and AI infrastructure. Asia Pacific, led by 
China and India, accounted for the majority of 
global electricity demand growth.
 The long-term outlook for the sector remains highly 
positive. Global electricity demand is expected to 
increase significantly through 2050, driven by the 
electrification of transport, industrial processes, 
cooling, and digital infrastructure. Emerging 
markets across Asia, the Middle East, and Africa 
are expected to play a pivotal role in this transition, 
creating significant long-term opportunities for 
integrated solar PV manufacturers with strong 
domestic supply chains.
3.2 India’s Renewable Energy Trajectory
 India’s renewable energy sector witnessed 
accelerated momentum in FY 2025-26, 
underpinned by the Government’s unwavering 
=== page 33 ===
Annual Report 2025-2630
commitment to achieving 500 GW of non-fossil 
fuel capacity by FY 2030. Key milestones achieved 
during FY 2025-26 include:
• India added a record 44.6 GW of solar capacity 
in FY 2025-26, representing 87% YoY growth – 
the highest annual solar addition in India’s 
history.
• Total installed solar capacity crossed 150 GW 
by March 2026, making India the world’s 3rd 
largest solar market (IRENA 2026).
• India’s total installed renewable energy 
capacity (including large hydro) reached 
approximately 233 GW by March 2026, 
representing approximately 49% of total 
installed generation capacity.
• The country added 55.3 GW of total non-
fossil capacity in FY 2025-26, and renewable 
energy’s share in peak demand reached 
51.5% in July 2025.
• India’s National Electricity Plan (NEP) targets 
609 GW total installed capacity by FY2032, 
with solar capacity projected to reach 364.6 
GW.
 India’s solar sector continues to benefit from 
strong policy support, cost-competitive tariffs, and 
a maturing domestic manufacturing ecosystem. 
Solar tariffs in most states are significantly below 
the cost of new coal-fired capacity, and the 
pipeline of utility-scale, rooftop, and agricultural 
solar projects remains robust.
3.3 Solar Manufacturing Policy Landscape
 FY 2025-26 was a defining year for solar 
manufacturing policy in India:
• ALMM List-I:  The Approved List of Models and 
Manufacturers (ALMM) for solar PV modules crossed 100 GW of enlisted manufacturing 
capacity during FY 2025-26, covering more 
than 100 manufacturers and strengthening 
domestic sourcing requirements for 
government-supported projects.
• ALMM List-II: In July 2025, MNRE published 
ALMM List-II for solar cells, with mandatory 
compliance effective June 01, 2026. As at 
April 30, 2026, the enlisted cell capacity 
reached 30.3 GW across 13 manufacturers 
(7th revision). The policy is expected to 
support domestic integrated manufacturers 
by encouraging local cell manufacturing and 
reducing dependence on imports.
• ALMM List-III: In March 2026, MNRE expanded 
the ALMM framework to cover solar wafers, 
backed by equivalent ingot-manufacturing 
capacity, extending mandatory domestic 
sourcing requirements further upstream 
in India’s solar PV value chain. The new 
requirements are scheduled to take effect 
from 1 June 2028.
• Manufacturing Capacity Surge: India's solar 
module manufacturing capacity expanded 
rapidly during FY 2025-26, reaching over 120 
GW by March 2026, compared with around 
38 GW in March 2024. This expansion was 
supported by the Production Linked Incentive 
(PLI) Scheme, Basic Customs Duty (BCD), 
and the growing emphasis on domestic 
manufacturing.
• Solar Cell Capacity: India's solar cell 
manufacturing capacity increased 
significantly during FY 2025-26, reaching 
approximately 27 GW, reflecting the growing 
emphasis on developing an integrated 
domestic solar manufacturing ecosystem.
3.4 Demand Drivers
 India’s solar sector is experiencing robust momentum, underpinned by a combination of improving techno-economic 
viability, strong policy support, and structural sectoral reforms. Key demand drivers include:
Demand-side Support Supply-side Support
• Renewable Consumption Obligation (RCO):  
Progressive increase in renewable energy 
consumption targets for obligated entities through FY 
2029-30 is expected to support sustained demand for 
renewable power and solar modules.
• PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana:  Residential rooftop 
subsidy scheme targeting 25-30 GW over three years, 
generating demand for domestically certified (DCR) 
modules.• Solar Park Scheme:  Continued development of 
large-scale solar parks is improving project readiness 
and facilitating utility-scale renewable energy 
deployment.
• Green Energy Corridor (GEC):  Continued 
investments in interstate and intrastate transmission 
infrastructure are strengthening grid integration for 
renewable energy and enabling higher renewable 
penetration.

solarisation initiatives for the agricultural sector 
is expected to drive demand for domestically 
manufactured solar modules and decentralised solar 
solutions.
• CPSU Scheme Phase-II:  Viability Gap Funding 
for PSU solar projects targeting 12 GW with DCR 
modules.
• Green Open Access Rules:  Simplified access norms 
and lower eligibility thresholds continue to accelerate 
renewable energy adoption among commercial and 
industrial consumers.• PLI Scheme for High-Efficiency Solar PV Modules:  
39,600 MW capacity allocated under Tranche-II to 11 
companies with approximately INR 93,000 Crores 
committed investment.
• BCD and AIDC Tariff Regime: 40% on modules, 25% 
on cells (plus AIDC surcharges) protects domestic 
manufacturers from Chinese dumping.
• Mandatory Energy Storage:  Recent bidding 
guidelines requiring energy storage for new 
renewable energy projects are expected to accelerate 
deployment of battery energy storage systems and 
improve grid reliability.
4.  COMPANY OVERVIEW
 Saatvik Green Energy Limited (SGEL) is one of India's leading solar photovoltaic (PV) module manufacturers, which was 
incorporated in 2015 and achieved a significant milestone with its listing on the National Stock Exchange of India (NSE) and 
BSE Limited (BSE) in September 2025. SGEL along with its group companies (“ Saatvik”) operates an integrated business 
model encompassing Solar PV module manufacturing, Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC) services, 
Operations & Maintenance (O&M), and power electronics solutions, including solar inverters. Its primary manufacturing 
facility is located in Ambala, Haryana, while a greenfield integrated manufacturing facility is being developed in Odisha.
 Since its inception, Saatvik has supplied over 5 GW of solar PV modules across domestic and international markets, 
catering to utility-scale, commercial & industrial (C&I), residential rooftop, agricultural solar, and other distributed energy  
applications.
 Saatvik is executing a phased capacity expansion strategy with a clear focus on backward integration and the development of a 
fully integrated clean energy manufacturing and solutions platform. The Company's integrated manufacturing project in Odisha 
is progressing as planned and will significantly enhance its manufacturing footprint. In line with its long-term vision, Saatvik 
has enhanced its planned solar cell manufacturing capacity from 4.8 GW to 6 GW and is also progressing towards establishing 
6 GW of ingot and wafer manufacturing capacity. These initiatives, together with the expansion of encapsulant manufacturing 
and other ancillary components, are expected to strengthen supply chain resilience, improve cost competitiveness, increase 
value addition, and reinforce the Company's presence across the solar manufacturing value chain.
 Looking ahead, Saatvik aims to progressively build an end-to-end integrated energy platform spanning solar manufacturing, 
storage solutions, power electronics, components, and energy infrastructure, positioning the Company to capitalise on the 
long-term opportunities arising from India's energy transition.
5.  FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE – FY 2025-26
 Saatvik delivered a strong financial performance in FY 2025-26, driven by robust growth in production and sales volumes, 
healthy capacity utilisation, and sustained demand for high-efficiency n-Type TOPCon modules. The table below presents the 
Company's consolidated financial highlights for the year.

 5.2 Financial & Operational Performance Overview
 FY 2025-26 was a landmark year for Saatvik, marked by record financial and operational performance. On Consolidated 
basis, Revenue from Operations increased by 111% year-on-year to INR 45,484 millions, driven by strong growth in 
production and sales volumes, healthy capacity utilisation, and sustained demand for high-efficiency n-Type TOPCon 
modules. EBITDA and Profit After Tax (PAT) grew by 62% and 64% year-on-year to INR 5,811 millions and INR 3,571 
millions, respectively. During the year, the Company produced 3,162 MW and sold 3,138 MW of solar modules while 
maintaining a healthy capacity utilisation of over 84%, reflecting efficient utilisation of manufacturing assets and strong 
execution capabilities.
 Q4 FY 2025-26 was the Company's strongest quarter of the year, with Revenue from Operations of INR 16,077 millions, 
module production of 935 MW, and module sales of 1,050 MW. The quarter reflected robust customer demand, timely 
execution of the Company's order book, and continued operational discipline. The Company also strengthened its 
market position through sustained investments in manufacturing expansion, backward integration, and product portfolio 
diversification, laying a strong foundation for future growth.
 While EBITDA and PAT margins moderated during FY 2025-26, primarily due to industry-wide pricing normalisation, 
elevated solar cell procurement costs, and higher operating expenses associated with capacity expansion, the Company 
continued to deliver healthy profitability and strong cash generation. The successful completion of the IPO during the 
year further strengthened the balance sheet, with the debt-to-equity ratio improving to 0.65x from 1.34x in FY 2024-25. 
Supported by a robust order book, expanding manufacturing capacities, and continued policy support for domestic solar 
manufacturing, Saatvik remains well positioned to capitalise on the long-term growth opportunities in India's renewable 
energy sector.

2024-25 following the successful IPO and prudent 
financial management. ROE and ROCE moderated 
during the year primarily due to the increase in 
shareholders' equity and higher capital employed 
resulting from ongoing capacity expansion. These 
investments have strengthened the Company's 
financial foundation to support long-term growth.
5.4 Business Momentum and Revenue Visibility
 The Company continued to witness robust demand 
across utility-scale, Commercial & Industrial (C&I), 
and government-led solar projects during FY 2025-
26, supported by favourable industry fundamentals 
and its established execution capabilities. A 
diversified customer base and strong market 
presence enabled Saatvik to maintain healthy 
order inflows throughout the year.
 As on March 31, 2026, the Company's confirmed 
order book stood at approximately 5.89 GW, 
providing strong revenue visibility for the coming 
quarters. The order book comprises a balanced 
mix of utility-scale, commercial & industrial, and government projects, supporting business stability 
while mitigating customer concentration risk.
 The robust order pipeline, coupled with the 
Company's ongoing manufacturing expansion, 
backward integration initiatives, and favourable 
policy environment, provides a strong foundation 
for sustained growth. These strategic initiatives 
position Saatvik well to capitalise on the long-term 
opportunities arising from India's accelerating 
energy transition.
6. OPERATIONAL PERFORMANCE
 Saatvik delivered strong operational performance during 
FY 2025-26, supported by robust demand, efficient 
manufacturing operations, and disciplined execution. 
The Company's Ambala manufacturing facility operated 
at a healthy capacity utilisation of 84.07%, reflecting 
efficient asset utilisation and sustained demand for its 
products. Total module production reached a record 
3,162 MW during the year, underscoring the Company's 
ability to successfully scale operations while maintaining 
manufacturing efficiency. The ongoing capacity 
expansion and backward integration initiatives are 
expected to further strengthen operational capabilities 
and support the Company's long-term growth strategy.

7.  STRATEGIC DEVELOPMENTS IN FY 2025-26
7.1 Initial Public Offering (IPO)
 FY 2025-26 marked a transformational year for 
Saatvik Green Energy Limited with its successful 
listing on the NSE and BSE in September 2025. 
The IPO resulted in the successful mobilisation 
of INR 7,000 millions, significantly strengthening 
the Company's capital structure and providing the 
financial resources to support its next phase of 
growth. The proceeds from the IPO are being deployed 
towards strategic growth initiatives, including 
manufacturing capacity expansion, backward 
integration across the solar value chain, debt 
reduction, and general corporate purposes. The 
listing has also enhanced the Company's corporate 
profile, strengthened governance standards, 
broadened its shareholder base, and improved 
access to capital markets, positioning Saatvik to 
capitalise on the long-term opportunities arising 
from India's accelerating energy transition.
7.2 Product Portfolio Diversification
 FY 2025-26 marked a significant milestone in 
Saatvik's strategy to expand beyond solar module 
manufacturing and strengthen its position as an 
integrated clean energy solutions provider. During 
the year, the Company broadened its product 
portfolio through the introduction of new offerings 
across power electronics and distributed solar 
solutions, enhancing its ability to address a wider 
spectrum of customer requirements.
 UDAY Series On-Grid Inverters : The Company 
launched the UDAY Series of on-grid solar 
inverters, offering capacities ranging from 1.1 
kW to 50 kW. The product portfolio incorporates 
advanced features, including high MPPT efficiency, 
multiple connectivity options, and IP65-rated 
protection, enabling Saatvik to offer integrated 
solar solutions comprising modules, inverters, 
and EPC services.
 Solar Pump Solutions:  The Company continued to 
strengthen its presence in the solar pump segment, 
supported by increasing adoption of solar-powered 
irrigation systems and government initiatives 
such as PM-KUSUM. This business complements 
Saatvik's expanding portfolio of distributed 
renewable energy solutions and provides access to 
a fast-growing market segment.
=== page 37 ===
Annual Report 2025-2634
7.3  Backward Integration & Supply Chain Resilience
 EPE Film Manufacturing: The commissioning of a 
2 GW in-house EPE film facility at Ambala marks an 
important step towards supply chain self-reliance. 
Encapsulant films are a critical component in 
high-performance solar PV modules, and in-house 
production reduces dependency on external 
suppliers (primarily Chinese imports), optimises 
costs, and enhances quality control.
 Odisha Cell Facility: The upcoming 6 GW solar  
cell manufacturing capacity in Odisha, once 
commissioned, will materially reduce Company’s 
dependency on imported cells (currently the 
primary cost input), improve gross margins, and 
align the Company with ALMM List-II requirements 
that become mandatory from June 2026.
7.4 Entry into Energy Storage Systems (BESS)
 As part of its long-term growth strategy, SGEL 
incorporated Saatvik Power Storage Solutions 
Limited, a wholly owned subsidiary focused on 
battery technologies and Battery Energy Storage 
Systems (BESS). This strategic initiative marks the 
Company's entry into the rapidly evolving energy 
storage segment, which is expected to play an 
increasingly important role in enabling higher 
renewable energy penetration and enhancing 
grid stability. The venture aligns with Saatvik's 
vision of building an integrated clean energy 
platform spanning solar manufacturing, energy 
storage, power electronics, and broader energy 
infrastructure.
7.5 Value Chain Expansion through Strategic 
Acquisition
 In  April 2026, Melcon’s acquisition, marks 
the Company's strategic entry into the power 
transmission equipment segment and 
complements its long-term vision of building an 
integrated clean energy platform. It is expected 
to enhance Saatvik's presence across the 
power infrastructure ecosystem while creating 
opportunities for product diversification and long-
term value creation.
8 GLOBAL ESG RECOGNITION
 In March 2026, SGEL awarded the Bronze Medal by 
EcoVadis, placing the Company in the 79th percentile 
among companies assessed globally for its sustainability 
performance. The recognition reflects Company’s continued commitment to responsible manufacturing, 
environmental stewardship, ethical business practices, 
and sustainable value creation.
9. OUTLOOK – FY 2026-27 AND BEYOND
 Saatvik enters FY 2026-27 with a strong strategic 
foundation, supported by an expanding manufacturing 
footprint, a robust order book, and favourable industry 
dynamics. The Company's key growth drivers include:
• Odisha Integrated Manufacturing Facility:  The 
phased commissioning of the Odisha project is 
expected to significantly enhance manufacturing 
capacity and strengthen the Company's integrated 
manufacturing capabilities across the solar value 
chain.
• Backward Integration:  The commissioning of 
solar cell manufacturing facilities and the planned 
development of ingot and wafer manufacturing 
are expected to improve supply chain resilience, 
increase value addition, and enhance long-term 
cost competitiveness.
• Robust Order Book:  As at March 31, 2026, 
the Company had a confirmed order book of 
approximately 5.89 GW, providing healthy revenue 
visibility for the coming quarters.
• Product Portfolio Expansion:  The launch of the 
UDAY Series of on-grid solar inverters, continued 
expansion of the solar pump business, and entry 
into Battery Energy Storage Systems (BESS) 
are expected to diversify revenue streams and 
strengthen Saatvik's integrated clean energy 
solutions portfolio.
• Strategic Diversification:  The acquisition of 
Melcon Transformers and Electricals Private 
Limited expands the Company's presence into 
the power transmission equipment segment, 
complementing its long-term strategy of building 
an integrated energy platform.
• Supportive Policy Environment:  Continued 
government support through initiatives such as 
ALMM, domestic manufacturing incentives, and 
renewable energy policies is expected to strengthen 
the operating environment for integrated domestic 
solar manufacturers.
 Supported by these strategic initiatives and India's 
accelerating energy transition, Saatvik remains well 
positioned to deliver sustainable long-term growth and 
create value for all stakeholders.

COMPANY OVERVIEW
Saatvik Green Energy Limited, together with its wholly owned 
subsidiary, Saatvik Solar Industries Private Limited (“SSIPL”), 
is among India’s leading manufacturers of solar photovoltaic 
=== page 40 ===
Saatvik Green Energy Limited37
Statutory Reports 1 2 3
CIN : L40106HR2015PLC075578(PV) modules. As of March 31, 2026, the Company has 
a combined operational manufacturing capacity of 4.8 
gigawatts (GW). Over the years, the Company has established 
a strong presence in India’s solar energy sector, driven by its 
focus on superior product quality, advanced technology, and 
robust execution capabilities.
The Company has supplied over 5 GW of high-efficiency 
solar PV modules across both domestic and international 
markets. In addition to its core manufacturing operations, the 
Company offers integrated solutions, including Engineering, 
Procurement and Construction (EPC) services, as well as 
Operations and Maintenance (O&M) services.
Its product portfolio includes advanced technologies 
such as Mono PERC and N-TOPCon modules, available in  
mono-facial and bifacial configurations, catering to a wide 
range of applications across government, commercial, 
industrial, and residential segments.
The Company currently operates three solar module 
manufacturing facilities located in Ambala, Haryana, which 
together constitute one of the largest single-location solar 
module manufacturing hubs in India. These facilities are 
equipped with fully automated production lines, ensuring 
high precision, consistent quality, and enhanced operational 
efficiency.
CREDIT RATING
CRISIL Ratings reviewed and upgraded the ratings assigned 
to the Company’s banking facilities. The Long-Term Bank 
Facilities were upgraded to CRISIL A-/Stable , while the Short-
Term Bank Facilities were assigned a rating of CRISIL A2+ . 
The upgraded ratings reflect the Company’s strong business 
fundamentals, healthy financial profile, robust liquidity 
position, and demonstrated ability to meet its debt servicing 
and other financial obligations in a timely manner.
UPDATE ON SIGNIFICANT MATTERS
Initial Public Offer and Listing of Equity Shares
During the year under review, your Company achieved a 
significant milestone with the successful completion of its 
Initial Public Offering (“IPO”) and the subsequent listing of its 
Equity Shares on BSE Limited (“BSE”) and the National Stock 
Exchange of India Limited (“NSE”) on September 26, 2025. The 
listing represents an important step in the Company’s growth 
journey, enhancing its capital base, strengthening corporate 
governance standards, and providing greater visibility among 
investors and other stakeholders.
In connection with the IPO, your Company filed the Draft 
Red Herring Prospectus (“DRHP”), Updated Red Herring 
Prospectus (“UDRHP”), Red Herring Prospectus (“RHP”) 
and Prospectus with the Securities and Exchange Board 
of India (“SEBI”), the Stock Exchanges and the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (“MCA”) in accordance with the applicable 
provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI (Issue 
of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2018, 
as amended. The IPO comprised a Fresh Issue aggregating 
to INR 7,000 millions and an Offer for Sale aggregating to 
INR 2,000 millions by the Selling Shareholders, namely  
Mr. Parmod Kumar and Mrs. Sunila Garg, taking the total offer 
size to INR 9,000 millions. The Offer consisted of 1,93,59,079 
Equity Shares of face value INR 2 each at an Offer Price of 
INR 465 per Equity Share, including a premium of INR 463 
per Equity Share. Eligible employees participating under the 
Employee Reservation Portion were offered a discount of  
INR 44 per Equity Share on the Offer Price.

Additionally, a separate statement containing the salient

position of each subsidiary. The audited financial statements,

performance of the Company’s subsidiaries is provided as

=== page 41 ===
Annual Report 2025-2638
(i) Saatvik Solar Industries Private Limited (“SSIPL”)
 Saatvik Solar Industries Private Limited (“SSIPL”), a 
wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, has been 
identified as a material subsidiary under the SEBI Listing 
Regulations, underscoring its strategic importance and 
central role in the Group’s growth trajectory.
 SSIPL serves as the manufacturing backbone of the 
Group, producing high-efficiency solar photovoltaic 
modules and, through backward integration, solar 
cells. It is spearheading the Group’s flagship greenfield 
expansion at Gopalpur, Odisha, comprising an 
integrated 2.4 GW solar cell and 4.0 GW module facility 
under Phase I, followed by an additional 3.6 GW of solar 
cell capacity under Phase II. The project is expected 
to significantly enhance the Group’s manufacturing 
scale and strengthen its position among India’s leading 
vertically integrated solar manufacturers.
 SSIPL has reported a total revenue of INR 39,479.68 
millions for the current year as compared to  
INR 11,869.15 millions in the previous year. The total 
comprehensive income for the year under review 
amounted to INR 3,003.59 millions as compared to 
income of INR 683.12 millions in the previous year.
(ii) Saatvik Cleantech EPC Private Limited(“EPC”)
 Saatvik Cleantech EPC Private Limited(“EPC”), a 
wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, extends the 
Group’s footprint across the solar value chain beyond 
manufacturing.
 EPC delivers end-to-end Engineering, Procurement and 
Construction of solar power projects — from utility-
scale solar parks to solar water-pumping systems 
under national schemes such as PM-KUSUM — 
covering design, civil works, installation, testing and 
commissioning. During the year, it also took the Group’s 
first step into power generation by agreeing to acquire 
a stake in Intelligent Hydel Solutions Private Limited, 
reinforcing Saatvik’s evolution into a fully integrated 
renewable energy platform.
 EPC has reported total revenue of INR 621.88 millions 
for the current year as compared to INR 735.49 millions 
in the previous year. The Total comprehensive income for 
the year under review amounted to INR 22.93 millions as 
compared to income of INR 4.88 millions in the previous 
year.
(iii) Saatvik Green Energy USA Inc. (“SGEL-USA”)
 Saatvik Green Energy USA Inc. (“SGEL-USA”), a wholly 
owned foreign subsidiary of the Company, established 
in April 2023 and headquartered in Houston, Texas, 
marking the Group’s first step toward global expansion.
 SGEL-USA is the Company’s gateway to the American 
market, distributing high-quality, made-in-India Saatvik solar PV modules to customers across the United States. 
US being one of the world’s largest solar market with 
typically higher realisations than at domestic market, 
it is expected to play a growing role in diversifying the 
Group’s revenue base as manufacturing capacity scales 
up.
(iv) Saatvik Power Storage Solutions Limited (“SPSSL”)
 Saatvik Power Storage Solutions Limited (“SPSSL”) was 
incorporated on March 09, 2026 as a wholly owned 
subsidiary of the Company, marking the Group’s formal 
entry into the energy storage business.
 SPSSL is into the business of designing and deploy 
battery energy storage systems across utility-
scale, commercial and industrial, and residential 
applications, with the Group targeting up to 20 GW of 
energy storage capacity over the next five years. The 
subsidiary completes the Group’s integrated clean 
energy ecosystem — spanning manufacturing, EPC 
and storage — positioning Saatvik to enable reliable, 
round-the-clock renewable power.
(v)  Melcon Transformers and Electricals Private Limited 
(“Melcon”)
 The Company has also acquired an 80% equity stake 
in Melcon Transformers and Electricals Private Limited 
pursuant to a Share Purchase Agreement dated April 23, 
2026, making it a subsidiary of the Company.
 Melcon is an ISO 9001-certified, Jaipur-based 
manufacturer of power and distribution transformers, 
with a product range spanning oil-type, dry-type, 
auxiliary and energy-efficient units up to 12,500 
kVA. The acquisition marks the Company’s strategic 
entry into transmission and distribution equipment 
manufacturing, deepening its control across the power 
value chain as it evolves from a solar manufacturer into 
a fully integrated energy solutions provider.
(vi) Intelligent Hydel Solutions Private Limited(“IHSPL”) – 
Step Down Subsidiary
 During the year, Saatvik Cleantech EPC Private Limited, 
a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, has acquired  
49% equity stake in Intelligent Hydel Solutions Private 
Limited along with management control rights, making 
IHSPL a subsidiary of Saatvik Cleantech EPC Private 
Limited w.e.f. March 17, 2026.
 IHSPL holds a Power Purchase Agreement with 
Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Company 
Limited for a 41 MW AC solar power project in the state, 
developed under its programme to solarise agricultural 
and rural feeders. The investment marks the Group’s 
first step into the Independent Power Producer segment, 
adding long-term, PPA-backed generation assets to its 
manufacturing and EPC businesses.

EVALUATION OF BOARD, ITS COMMITTEES AND DIRECTORS
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee of the Board 
has laid down a comprehensive framework for the evaluation 
of the performance of the Individual Directors, the Board and 
its Committee(s). The evaluation criteria are broadly aligned 
with the Guidance Note on Board Evaluation issued by the 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI). 
Evaluation of Directors 
The performance evaluation of Individual Directors, inter alia, 
includes assessment of their knowledge and competence, 
fulfilment of fiduciary and statutory duties, ability to function 
as an effective team member, initiative and engagement, 
availability and attendance at meetings, commitment, 
integrity and independence. It also covers their contribution 
and participation in Board and Committee meetings, as 
well as guidance and support provided to the management 
outside such meetings. 
In addition, the Chairman is evaluated on specific aspects 
of his role, including effectiveness of leadership, ability to 
steer meetings constructively and impartially, consideration 
of Shareholders’ interests, and the ability to mentor, motivate 
and provide guidance to the Executive Directors. 
Evaluation of Board
The Board evaluation includes, inter alia, assessment of the 
Board’s composition and structure, effectiveness of meetings 
and overall functioning, discharge of key responsibilities, 
delegation of authority to Committees, effectiveness of Board 
processes, adequacy and timeliness of information, and the 
quality of interaction and relationship between the Board and 
the management.

Annual Report 2025-2642
Evaluation of Board Committee(s)
The evaluation of Committee(s) encompasses, inter alia, 
their mandate and composition, effectiveness in discharging 
assigned responsibilities, structure and frequency of 
meetings, independence from the Board, contribution to 
Board decision-making, and the quality of interaction with the 
Board and the management. 
Evaluation Process
The Board evaluated the effectiveness of its functioning, of 
the Committees and of individual Directors, pursuant to the 
provisions of the Act and the SEBI Listing Regulations. Based 
on the Guidance Note on Board Evaluation issued by the 
Securities and Exchange Board of India the Board Evaluation 
was carried out on following parameters, namely:
•	 Composition and calibre of the Board;
•	 Strategic direction and performance appraisal;
•	 Comprehension of business operations, risk 
management, processes, and protocols;
•	 Value creation for stakeholders and commitment to 
responsibilities;
•	 Supervision of financial reporting, internal controls, and 
auditing functions;
•	 Ethical standards, compliance, and oversight activities
The Board evaluation process for the year 2025-26 was 
conducted in a systematic and comprehensive manner. 
A structured questionnaire covering various aspects of 
the Board’s functioning, such as Board composition and 
dynamics, Board’s oversight and governance, Board strategy 
and performance, Board development and culture, etc., was 
circulated to all the Directors and feedback was sought on the 
same. 
During a separate meeting of the Independent Directors 
held on March 20, 2026, a comprehensive evaluation was 
conducted on the performance of the Non-Independent 
Directors, the Board as a whole, and the Chairman, 
incorporating feedback from the Executive Directors and 
other Non-Executive Directors. The NRC also assessed 
the performance of individual Directors and the Board 
collectively. In the subsequent Board meeting, which 
followed the Independent Directors’ meeting and the NRC 
meeting, the performance of the Board, its committees, and 
individual Directors, including the Chairman, was thoroughly 
discussed. The Board evaluation for the financial year  
2025-26 was completed, with key findings and 
recommendations noted for ongoing improvement.
FAMILIARISATION PROGRAMME FOR INDEPENDENT 
DIRECTORS 
The familiarisation programme is tailored to align with each 
Director’s areas of interest and expertise. This programme is 
carefully structured to comply with statutory requirements under the Act and other relevant regulations. As part of this 
initiative, the Directors are encouraged to visit the Company’s 
facilities, providing them with an opportunity to observe 
operations first-hand and engage directly with members of 
Senior Management. These plant visits help to foster a deeper 
appreciation of the Company’s processes, culture, and 
strategic priorities.
In addition, the induction programmes includes a series of 
detailed presentations delivered by Senior Management. 
These presentations cover a wide range of topics, including 
the Company’s corporate strategy, operational framework, 
product portfolio, market presence, group structure 
and subsidiaries, composition of the Board, governance 
guidelines, matters reserved specifically for Board decision, 
and the approach to risk identification and mitigation.
Through this multi-faceted orientation, Directors gain 
valuable insights into the Company’s core values, business 
drivers, and leadership approach. This comprehensive 
understanding enables them to contribute more meaningfully 
during Board deliberations and to exercise effective oversight 
of management performance, ultimately supporting the 
Company’s long-term success.
Further, the details of the familiarisation programme provided 
to the Directors is available on the website of the Company 
at https://www.saatvikgroup.com/docs/2026/08/Saatvik-
Familiarisation-Programme-for-IDs.pdf

The Company has adopted a Nomination and Remuneration

philosophy, and the basis for payment of remuneration to

RISK MANAGEMENT 
The Company is aware of the importance of identifying risks 
and opportunities that may affect its operations or growth. At 
present, the Company has established a comprehensive risk 
management framework to proactively identify, assess, and 
manage risks and opportunities that could potentially impact 
the Company’s operations or performance. The Company 
follows a practical and ongoing approach to monitor key risks 
and opportunities and to frame and implement mitigation 
strategies for identified risks.
The Board has formed a Risk Management Committee (RMC) 
to frame, implement and monitor the risk management plan 
for your Company. The RMC is responsible for reviewing 
the risk management plan and ensuring its effectiveness. 
The Audit Committee has additional oversight in the area of 
financial risks and controls. The major risks identified by the 
businesses are systematically addressed through mitigation 
actions on a continual basis. Further details on the Risk 
Management activities, including the implementation of risk 
management policy, key risks identified and their mitigations 
are covered in Management Discussion and Analysis Report, 
which forms part of this Annual Report.
The Management keeps the Board of Directors informed 
about any major risks that could affect the business, such as 
operational risks, credit risks and legal and regulatory risks 
from time to time.

We have below general remarks with regard to our report:
	 It is suggested to strengthen the internal controls for

Company, particularly in respect of recording of UPSI 
events in the SDD.
	 During the review period, a wholly owned subsidiary 
(‘WOS’) of the Company entered a related party 
transaction (RPT) with its subsidiary (not a WOS) 
without obtaining the required prior approval from the 
Company’s Audit Committee. Because the transaction 
value exceeded the threshold permitted for post-facto 
ratification by the Audit Committee, the matter was 
subsequently escalated to the Board of Directors. 
Following a review and consideration of the Audit 
Committee’s views, the Board formally approved the 
transaction within three months of its execution.
Management Response : Prior approval could not be obtained 
as the step-down subsidiary was acquired recently (effective 
March 17, 2026), and the transaction was inadvertently 
missed during the initial related party identification process 
due to an oversight. Consequently, post-facto approval was 
sought from and granted by the Board of Directors, acting on 
the recommendation of the Audit Committee. Management 
confirms that the transaction was conducted in the ordinary 
course of business and on an arm’s length basis.

standards.

Others

(B) TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION
i. The efforts made towards 
technology absorptionEffectively implemented the N-TOPCon M10R & G12R cell technology to developed new 
higher wattage solar modules. These initiatives demonstrate our dedication to adopting 
internationally acknowledged developments in photovoltaic (PV) technology. The power 
output and temperature coefficients of the new module series (600 Wp using M10R and 
625 Wp using G12R) show notable improvements.
The said product is successfully tested at third part lab for product quality, performance 
and reliability. These product are also certified under Domestic (BIS) and International 
(IEC, UL and CE) standards. This ensures our products meet the stringent quality and 
safety requirements for both Indian and global markets.
These development has resulted into extension of lifespan and designed to survive the 
harsh Indian climate and grid conditions, which include high temperatures, humidity, 
dust.
ii. The benefits derived Adoption of N TOPCon M10R and G12R cells enabled the development of 600Wp and 625 
Wp modules respectively, providing higher power output in the same module footprint 
compared to conventional half-cut PERC-based modules.
The N-TOPCon M10R modules work better in low light and have greater conversion 
efficiencies (over 22.5%).
Particularly in utility-scale projects with land and BOS (Balance of System) restrictions, 
higher efficiency modules immediately result in reduced Levelised Cost of Energy (LCOE) 
for end users.
With a lower temperature coefficient of Pmax (~-0.30%/°C) than PERC(~-0.55%/°C), 
N-TOPCon technology is more suited for hotter regions like India.
Compared to bifacial installations, TOPCon cells' bifaciality factor (~80%) guarantees a 
higher energy production, especially on reflecting ground surfaces.
These products has helped the Company to be a top manufacturer in market and offering 
the latest technology products to its customer.
iii. In case of import technology:
	 Details of technology 
imported
	 Year of import
	 Whether the technology 
been fully absorbedNA

iv. Expenditure incurred on 
Research and Development 
(R & D)Approximately INR 10 Crores towards the development and validation of new solar 
module.
The investment was made in the design and prototyping of advanced module 
technologies, including products based on G12R and M10R N-TOPCon cells, targeted at 
delivering higher power outputs (up to 625Wp). Engineering and process teams worked 
on optimising interconnection designs, layout configurations, and module mechanical 
stability.
A substantial portion of the R&D expenditure was allocated for third-party testing and 
certification of modules under various Domestic (BIS) and International (IEC, UL, CE) 
standards. This ensures our products meet the stringent quality and safety requirements 
for both Indian and global markets.
The Company commissioned an in-house Reliability and Validation Laboratory, equipped 
with environmental chambers, mechanical load testers, PID and LID testing setups. This 
facility enhances our ability to conduct accelerated stress testing and simulate real-
world conditions for final product evaluation.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT
COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CORPORATE GOVERNANCE
Corporate governance is regarded as the cornerstone 
of sustainable value creation and responsible business 
conduct. Your Company is committed to upholding 
the highest standards of governance, transparency, 
accountability, integrity, and ethical business practices 
across all aspects of its operations. Its governance 
framework is structured to promote long-term  
stakeholder confidence while ensuring strict adherence to 
applicable laws, regulations, and recognised industry best 
practices.
As a leading player in the renewable energy sector, your 
Company recognises that sound corporate governance 
is integral to achieving sustainable growth, enhancing 
operational excellence, and delivering enduring value to 
shareholders and other stakeholders. Your Company’s 
governance philosophy is anchored in its commitment to 
responsible business practices, prudent risk management, 
transparency in disclosures, and adherence to the highest 
ethical standards.
GOVERNANCE STRUCTURE
Your Company’s governance structure is guided by its Vision, 
Mission, and Core Values, which emphasise innovation, 
sustainability, operational excellence, and stakeholder 
engagement. Through a well-defined framework of policies, 
systems, and processes, the Company seeks to balance 
entrepreneurial leadership with robust oversight and 
accountability.
Your Company has adopted a comprehensive Code of 
Conduct applicable to all Directors and employees, including 
Independent Directors, which reflects the principles of 
integrity, ethical behaviour, transparency, and accountability. 
The Code incorporates the duties and responsibilities 
prescribed under the Companies Act, 2013 and the 
SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations”).
Your Company has also established a robust governance 
framework comprising various Board Committees, policies, 
internal control mechanisms, risk management processes, 
and compliance systems to facilitate effective decision-
making and oversight. The framework promotes diversity of 
thought, independence in decision-making, and continuous 
evaluation of governance practices in line with evolving 
regulatory and stakeholder expectations.
Your Company remains committed to continually 
strengthening its governance standards and practices, 
thereby reinforcing stakeholder confidence and supporting its long-term objective of creating sustainable value through 
responsible growth.
Your Company is in compliance with the corporate governance 
requirements specified under Regulations 17 to 27 read with 
Schedule V and clauses (b) to (i) and (t) of Regulation 46(2) of 
the SEBI Listing Regulations, as applicable.
BOARD OF DIRECTORS
The Board of Directors serves as the apex governing body of 
the Company and provides strategic direction, leadership, 
and oversight to ensure sustainable growth and long-term 
value creation. The Board is responsible for setting the 
Company’s strategic objectives, monitoring performance, 
and guiding management while safeguarding the interests of 
shareholders and other stakeholders.
The statutory and other significant and material information is 
placed before the Board to enable it to discharge its fiduciary 
duties keeping in mind the interests of all the stakeholders 
and the Company’s corporate governance philosophy. In the 
context of Company’s business and the industry in which the 
Company operates, the Board members have the appropriate 
skills/area of expertise/competencies.
Comprising individuals with diverse expertise and experience, 
the Board promotes a culture of integrity, accountability, 
transparency, and ethical conduct. It oversees the Company’s 
governance framework, risk management practices, internal 
controls, and compliance mechanisms, ensuring adherence 
to applicable laws, regulations, and governance standards in 
both letter and spirit.
Composition of Board 
The Board of the Company comprises individuals of high 
standing and professional eminence, with a balanced mix of 
skills, expertise, knowledge, and experience that enables it to 
effectively discharge its responsibilities and provide strategic 
leadership to the business.
The Board includes both Executive and Non-Executive 
Directors, comprising half of the Board as Independent 
Directors, including Independent Woman Director. As part 
of the Board’s ongoing succession planning process, the 
composition of the Board is reviewed periodically to ensure 
continued alignment with the Company’s strategy, long-term 
objectives, and the Board-approved skill matrix.
As of March 31, 2026, the Board has six Directors. Out 
of these, two are Promoter Executive Directors, one is a 
Promoter Non-Executive Director, and three are Independent 
Directors, including one Independent Woman Director. This 
composition follows the rules laid down in the relevant Act 
and the SEBI Listing Regulations.
